# Evaluation report 2026-10-20

> **Universe: point-in-time NIFTY50.** A stock is scored only on dates when it was an index member (`index_membership`, changes effective on or before the date); names that left the index count for the periods they were members. OOS coverage (members with a TECH_SCORE / members, per date): mean 76.6%, minimum 32.0% (1998-08-03). **Coverage is below 100%:** members without usable price history (or still in TECH_SCORE warmup) are left out on those dates, so a residual survivorship bias remains (no usable prices during the OOS window: BSE.NS, ETERNAL.NS, GAIL.NS, HDFC.NS, IBULHSGFIN.NS, INDIGO.NS, INFRATEL.NS, IOC.NS, ITCHOTELS.NS, JIOFIN.NS, KWIL.NS, LTIM.NS, MAXHEALTH.NS, SHREECEM.NS, TMCV.NS, UPL.NS, VEDL.NS, YESBANK.NS, ZEEL.NS). 5147 OOS dates fall before 2019-03-29 and use today's active stocks.

Generated by `python main.py evaluate` (`investbot/evaluation/`). Nothing is fitted: every signal is evaluated with its designed polarity on walk-forward test blocks only.

## Setup

- Universe: point-in-time NIFTY50 from `index_membership`: 71 names were members at some point since 2019-01-01; 51 have enough daily bars to compute features (on their full stored history; a signal counts only on dates with `IN_UNIVERSE`). Bars 1991-01-02 → 2026-10-19.
- Coverage on OOS dates (members with a finite TECH_SCORE / members): mean 76.6%, minimum 32.0% on 1998-08-03, over the OOS dates with ≥ 10 covered members (the dates the metrics use; 16 OOS dates with fewer, e.g. purged sessions, are left out).
- Members with **no price rows** (need a human-run ingest; OOS member days in brackets): BSE.NS (5160), ETERNAL.NS (5531), GAIL.NS (494), HDFC.NS (1060), HINDPETRO.NS (0), IBULHSGFIN.NS (120), INDIGO.NS (5406), INFRATEL.NS (367), IOC.NS (742), ITCHOTELS.NS (26), JIOFIN.NS (5565), KWIL.NS (54), LTIM.NS (298), MAXHEALTH.NS (5406), SHREECEM.NS (630), TMCV.NS (22), UPL.NS (1234), VEDL.NS (327), YESBANK.NS (237), ZEEL.NS (367).
- Membership before 2019-03-29 is not sourced and falls back to today's active stocks: 6812 panel dates (5147 OOS).
- Comparison universe "active": the 45 active stocks with enough bars, no masking (the pre-170 behaviour), evaluated on the same walk-forward splits (the calendar comes from the index universe).
- Out-of-sample (OOS): 57 test blocks of 6 months, 1998-07-01 → 2026-10-19, 7013 dates. Expanding train window (≥ 252 bars from the first date with TECH_SCORE on ≥ 10 names), embargo of 20 bars before each block.
- Labels (total return, dividends credited on their ex-date bars, not reinvested within a period, untaxed): `FWD_TR_RET_h = (close[t+h] + D[t+2..t+h]) / open[t+1] − 1` (signal known after t's close, entry at the next open, so a dividend whose ex-date is the entry bar is not earned), h ∈ {1, 5, 20}. Close-to-close `FWD_TR_CC_RET_h = (close[t+h] + D[t+1..t+h]) / close[t] − 1` for comparison only. The held portfolio uses `FWD_TR_OO_RET_h = (open[t+1+h] + D[t+2..t+1+h]) / open[t+1] − 1` (open-to-open, so periods tile the timeline; an exit at an ex-date's open earns that dividend). D[a..b] = dividends per share on stored bars a..b. The price-only labels `FWD_RET_h = close[t+h] / open[t+1] − 1` and `FWD_OO_RET_h` are in the "Dividend effect" table only.
- Dividend coverage: 51 of 51 names have at least one dividend row with an ex-date in the panel window, 1747 dividend rows in total.
- Unadjusted splits (`quality.detect_unadjusted_splits`, history before the ex-date on the old share scale): BEL.NS 2017-09-28 (×1.1, 23 periods), ICICIBANK.NS 2017-06-20 (×1.1, 23 periods). Every label whose period spans the ex-date boundary is NaN: 46 `FWD_TR_RET_h` periods over all h (the other label columns are NaN on the matching periods). Re-adjusting that history is a human decision (`python main.py dq`).
- Reproducibility: `SCORER_VERSION` 4, git commit `c66952e`, DB fingerprint: max `price_daily.date` 2026-10-19, 322816 `price_daily` rows, 1856 `corporate_actions` rows.
- IC: daily cross-sectional Spearman; ICIR = mean / sd of daily IC (not annualised); t-stats use Newey-West standard errors with lag h − 1.
- Multiple testing: Benjamini–Hochberg over all 141 signal × horizon tests. **Promising** = BH q < 0.10 and the IC sign holds in ≥ 70% of test blocks.
- Risk-free rate for Sharpe and Sortino: 6.50% a year, a constant placeholder for the 91-day T-bill (master plan §9.1), not a dated series. VaR/CVaR are per holding period of h bars.
- Bootstrap intervals (held portfolio): stationary block bootstrap, 2000 resamples, 90% percentile intervals, seed 20261019 (the same for every row), mean block length max(1, round(n^(1/3))) periods, pre-registered (no Politis–White selection), none below 20 periods.
- Costs: round trip 0.320% of traded value = statutory charges and brokerage from `config/tax_regimes.py` on a ₹1 lakh order (0.270%) + slippage 0.025% per side.
- Runtime: 1461 s; peak RSS 1224 MB (features computed one asset at a time, once for both universes).
- Added runtime of the 6 polarity-variant columns: 1.8 s to compute them (signal and aggregation steps only, no indicator recompute), plus about 152 s of evaluation (their 12% share of the 48 evaluated columns × 1219 s in `evaluate`, both universes; an estimate, not timed per column).

## Verdict

- **h = 1:** TECH_SCORE mean IC +0.009 (ICIR +0.043, NW t +3.61, BH q 0.00), not significant. Best baseline BASE_LOWVOL_20 ICIR +0.154. No technical signal beats the baselines as designed. Promising only with the sign inverted: VAR_REGIME_POLARITY_MOMENTUM.
- **h = 5:** TECH_SCORE mean IC -0.014 (ICIR -0.061, NW t -2.94, BH q 0.01), not significant. Best baseline BASE_REV_5 ICIR +0.157. No technical signal beats the baselines as designed. Promising only with the sign inverted: TECH_SCORE_VOLUME, SIG_EMA_20_POS, SIG_EMA_50_POS, SIG_DI_SPREAD, SIG_AROON, SIG_MACD_HIST, SIG_ROC, SIG_RVOL_CONFIRM, SIG_SR_POSITION, VAR_OSC_MOMENTUM, VAR_OSC_MOMENTUM_MOMENTUM, VAR_REGIME_POLARITY, VAR_REGIME_POLARITY_MOMENTUM.
- **h = 20:** TECH_SCORE mean IC -0.017 (ICIR -0.074, NW t -2.01, BH q 0.08), not significant. Best baseline BASE_MOM_12_1 ICIR +0.095. No technical signal beats the baselines as designed. Promising only with the sign inverted: SIG_RVOL_CONFIRM, SIG_SR_POSITION.

## IC inference robustness (pre-registered)

The verdict above uses a Newey-West t of the per-date IC series with a fixed lag h − 1, which covers the label overlap but not any further persistence of the IC series (at h = 1 it is a plain t-test). Two autocorrelation-robust p-values were pre-registered before this run (task 340) and are BH-adjusted within the same families: **auto** = Newey-West at the Andrews (1991) AR(1) plug-in lag for the Bartlett kernel, never below h − 1 (lag-1 ACF clipped at ±0.97, lag capped at floor(0.25 n)); **boot** = stationary block bootstrap of the centred IC series (2000 draws, seed 20261020, mean block max(h, auto lag + 1, round(n^(1/3))) dates), two-sided. ACF = sample autocorrelation of the per-date IC series. The promising rule is the same (q < 0.10 and the block sign share ≥ 70%), only the q-value differs.

- Headline family (next-open, primary return): 141 tests; promising 27 (fixed lag), 27 (auto), 27 (boot); verdict changes 0 under auto, 0 under boot; median ACF1 of the IC series +0.643.
- Price-only comparison family: 141 tests; promising 27 (fixed lag), 27 (auto), 27 (boot); verdict changes 0 under auto, 0 under boot; median ACF1 of the IC series +0.643.

Headline family (next-open, primary return): every test promising under any rule, plus TECH_SCORE at every horizon.

Resolution of the bootstrap p-value: with 2000 draws p boot ≥ 1 / 2001 = 0.00050, so in a family of 141 tests the smallest attainable q boot is 141 / 2001 = 0.070, and q boot < 0.10 for the top-ranked test needs p boot < 0.00071, i.e. **no** draw as extreme as the observed mean (one such draw gives q 0.141). For the strongest test the boot verdict is therefore a 0-versus-1 count out of 2000 draws; read it as "at the resolution limit", not as evidence against the test. The draw count is pre-registered and was not changed.

**The verdict rule is unchanged**: `promising` (and every section above and below) still uses `ic_q` from the fixed lag h − 1. The auto and boot columns are a pre-registered robustness check, not a new rule; switching the rule is a separate decision.

Runtime of the robust step: 472.0 s for 564 tests (both universes, per-date IC series only).

## Cost reality check: top-quintile long-only, open→close per period (OOS)

Equal weight, rebalanced every h bars; net = gross − turnover × 0.320%. Universe = equal weight over all names in the same periods. Simple annualisation (mean period return × 252 / h); not compounded, no taxes. Each period runs from open[t+1] to close[t+h], so the overnight gap before the next entry is not held: absolute returns at short horizons are mostly the (negative) open-to-close drift. Compare against the universe column, not against zero.

## Held portfolio (open-to-open, compounded)

The same top-quintile equal-weight portfolio, rebalanced every h bars, but held continuously: each period runs open[t+1] → open[t+1+h], so consecutive periods tile the OOS timeline and the overnight gap is included. Net = gross − turnover × 0.320% per period, compounded. CAGR over the invested periods (n × h / 252 years); vol = sd of net period returns × √(252 / h); max DD = worst peak-to-trough of the compounded net curve; IR = mean / sd of (net − universe) period returns × √(252 / h). CAGR, vol and IR use no risk-free rate: the benchmark is the equal-weight universe over the same periods. The OOS blocks are contiguous, so the curve spans the whole OOS window. No taxes.

| signal | h | periods | turnover | CAGR gross | CAGR net | CAGR universe | vol /yr | max DD | IR vs universe | Sharpe net | Sharpe univ. | Sortino | Calmar | max DD days | CVaR95 /period |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Risk-adjusted columns use the periods above: Sharpe and Sortino are annualised (× √(252 / h)) against the risk-free rate in the Setup (Sortino's downside target is that rate), Calmar = CAGR net / |max DD|, max DD days = bars from the peak before the worst drawdown until the curve is back at it (`*` = not recovered by the end of the window, counted to the end), CVaR95 = mean loss of the worst 5% of holding periods (a loss over h bars, not a daily one). These are cost-only signal portfolios without taxes: the §9.2 go/no-go thresholds apply to the full after-tax strategy, so no verdict is given here.

Each row resamples its own held-portfolio periods (net and universe jointly, the same indices for both) with a stationary block bootstrap (Politis & Romano 1994; 2000 resamples, mean block = round(n^(1/3)) periods, circular), seed 20261019 for every row; percentile intervals, "–" with fewer than 20 periods. The intervals are **per row and not BH-adjusted**: with this many rows, about 10% of the intervals exclude 0 by chance even when nothing works, so the Benjamini–Hochberg verdict above stays the headline. Calmar intervals at h = 20 rest on about 34 periods and are descriptive.

## Polarity variants (pre-registered)

Alternatives to the production oscillator polarity (follow momentum, fade to 0 at the extremes), fixed by Ankit's 2026-10-18 decision before any results were seen and not tuned (`investbot/evaluation/variants.py`): **VAR_OSC_REVERT** full mean reversion (`osc_extreme = −1`: a pinned oscillator is a sell); **VAR_OSC_MOMENTUM** pure momentum (`osc_extreme = +1`, no fade); **VAR_REGIME_POLARITY** production signals with every oscillator-rule signal (RSI, STOCH, WILLR, CCI, ULTOSC, BB_POS, KC_POS, MFI) negated on range-regime bars (revert in range, follow momentum otherwise). Each is aggregated with the production weights, regime blend and multiplier; `_MOMENTUM` is its momentum-family score, compared with TECH_SCORE_MOMENTUM. Same walk-forward blocks, labels and Benjamini–Hochberg family as every other signal (141 tests), on the point-in-time index universe. Held CAGR net / IR: the open-to-open top-quintile portfolio (see above). **Beats** = promising as designed (q < 0.10, block sign ≥ 70%, IC > 0) with an ICIR above both production and the best baseline.

- **VAR_OSC_REVERT_MOMENTUM** (vs TECH_SCORE_MOMENTUM): beats production and the best baseline at no horizon (h = 1 ΔICIR +0.021, Δ held CAGR net +3.65%; h = 5 ΔICIR +0.052, Δ held CAGR net +5.76%; h = 20 ΔICIR +0.022, Δ held CAGR net -0.21%).
- **VAR_OSC_MOMENTUM** (vs TECH_SCORE): beats production and the best baseline at no horizon (h = 1 ΔICIR -0.002, Δ held CAGR net -0.08%; h = 5 ΔICIR -0.004, Δ held CAGR net -1.03%; h = 20 ΔICIR +0.000, Δ held CAGR net -0.22%).
- **VAR_OSC_MOMENTUM_MOMENTUM** (vs TECH_SCORE_MOMENTUM): beats production and the best baseline at no horizon (h = 1 ΔICIR -0.010, Δ held CAGR net +1.19%; h = 5 ΔICIR -0.016, Δ held CAGR net -0.84%; h = 20 ΔICIR -0.005, Δ held CAGR net -2.60%).
- **VAR_REGIME_POLARITY** (vs TECH_SCORE): beats production and the best baseline at no horizon (h = 1 ΔICIR -0.038, Δ held CAGR net -3.06%; h = 5 ΔICIR -0.013, Δ held CAGR net -2.28%; h = 20 ΔICIR +0.000, Δ held CAGR net -2.32%).
- **VAR_REGIME_POLARITY_MOMENTUM** (vs TECH_SCORE_MOMENTUM): beats production and the best baseline at no horizon (h = 1 ΔICIR -0.096, Δ held CAGR net -0.56%; h = 5 ΔICIR -0.049, Δ held CAGR net +0.81%; h = 20 ΔICIR -0.016, Δ held CAGR net -2.12%).

Production polarity is unchanged. Whether to switch it to a variant is Ankit's decision (docs/DECISIONS_PENDING.md, "Polarity variants: harness results").

## By regime (OOS)

Cross-section restricted to names in that regime on that date. Daily IC and quintiles need ≥ 10 names on a date, so chaotic is mostly empty. The **pooled IC** uses every date with ≥ 3 names: per-date ranks scaled to [−0.5, 0.5], pooled over all (date, name) rows (larger cross-sections weigh more); its t-stat is Newey-West (lag h − 1) on the per-date sums.

## Caveats

- **Universe: point-in-time NIFTY50.** A stock is scored only on dates when it was an index member (`index_membership`, changes effective on or before the date); names that left the index count for the periods they were members. OOS coverage (members with a TECH_SCORE / members, per date): mean 76.6%, minimum 32.0% (1998-08-03). **Coverage is below 100%:** members without usable price history (or still in TECH_SCORE warmup) are left out on those dates, so a residual survivorship bias remains (no usable prices during the OOS window: BSE.NS, ETERNAL.NS, GAIL.NS, HDFC.NS, IBULHSGFIN.NS, INDIGO.NS, INFRATEL.NS, IOC.NS, ITCHOTELS.NS, JIOFIN.NS, KWIL.NS, LTIM.NS, MAXHEALTH.NS, SHREECEM.NS, TMCV.NS, UPL.NS, VEDL.NS, YESBANK.NS, ZEEL.NS). 5147 OOS dates fall before 2019-03-29 and use today's active stocks.
- Five years of daily history leave about three years out of sample after warmup (EMA_200, regime window) and the first training year; ~50 names per date.
- A missing session (purged bar) is skipped, not filled: t+1 is the next *stored* bar.
- Total return credits gross cash dividends; dividend tax (slab rate) is not modelled, and neither is STCG/LTCG on the trades.
- A signal significant only with its sign inverted is a finding about polarity, not something to switch on: polarity decisions go to Ankit with this evidence.
